The following construction replaces the secret-based finish using actual
extracted witnesses.

\subsection{Historical component: Finishing from extracted ballot nonces}\label{sec:extracted-finish}
The reduction's inputs are $g,pk,A,T$, preparation and the adversary's callbacks.
It runs the actual repeated prefix extractor using the pinned uniform-query
sampler, retains its original prefix, complete state and live cache, then
publishes trustee proofs and calls the original guessing callback.
The secret
is absent from this computation.
No branch is conditioned on extraction success.

\paragraph{Source-derived board and shares.}
Let $K_i$ be the prefix's retained honest column nonce sum.
Supported honest
ballot generation returns its supplied ciphertext fields.
When both honest
submissions accept, their actual board entries therefore give
\[
  \operatorname{boardTally}(\mathit{before.board})_i
   =\operatorname{Enc}_{g,pk}(K_i,\mathbf{1}_{i=0}).
\]
This is derived from the actual prefix, including later casting queries and
malicious submission; board presentation and freshness are not caller premises.
The malicious submission appends its ballot exactly on acceptance and preserves
the honest board for either rejection reason.
Initial and successor consistency
are derived from execution.
\companchor{ElectionDDHSource}{ElectionDDHSource.ballots_columns}
\companchor{ElectionDDHPrefix}{ElectionDDHSource.prefix_honest_columns}
\companchor{ElectionDDHPrefix}{ElectionDDHSource.prefix_cast_board}
\companchor{ElectionProgrammedSource}{ElectionProgrammedSource.raw_support}

For extracted component nonces $r_i$, finishing computes
\[
 D_i=\begin{cases}(K_i+r_i)\,pk,&\text{malicious acceptance and extraction success},\\
                  K_i\,pk,&\text{otherwise}.
     \end{cases}
\]
An accepted ballot without extraction uses an explicit fallback; correctness is
not asserted on that branch.
If both honest ballots accept and the accepted
malicious ballot has extracted witnesses, these shares equal the secret-based
shares for the actual retained tally.
Repeated-extractor success supplies the
witnesses for the original submission.
The resulting finishing source, including
proof generation and guessing, is exactly the secret-based continuation on
these good retained executions.
\companchor{ElectionDDHExtractedFinish}{ElectionDDHSource.prefix_extracted_shares}
\companchor{ElectionDDHExtractedFinish}{ElectionDDHSource.extracted_finish_good}

\begin{theorem}[Complete extracted finishing, machine-checked]\label{thm:extracted-finish}
Let $F$ be a finite field with its sampler and decidable equality, and let $G$
be an additive $F$-module with decidable equality. Write $q=|F|$. Fix $g\ne0$,
$sk\in F$ and arbitrary $A,T\in G$; put $pk=sk\,g$. Suppose preparation has at
most $p$ live ballot-hash queries and every casting callback has at most $c$.
For any $k\in\mathbb N$ and $\delta\in\mathbb R_{\ge0}^{\infty}$, set $N=p+c+9$.
Let $E_{\mathrm{ext}}$ run $k$ residual extraction attempts with bound $N$, then
finish with the public-input shares above. Let $E_{\mathrm{secret}}$ run the
same original prefix and finish using $sk$. If $R$ is the actual probability
that the original prefix's two honest decisions are not both accepting, then
\[
 \operatorname{ofReal}\!\left(\operatorname{TV}
   (E_{\mathrm{ext}},E_{\mathrm{secret}})\right)
 \le R+3(p+c+10)\delta+
       \left(1-(\delta-q^{-1})^3\right)^k.
\]
Subtraction on the right is truncated in $\mathbb R_{\ge0}^{\infty}$.
Both outputs retain the full public result, winning bit, complete private state
and original live cache. Later guessing queries are unrestricted. No acceptance,
extraction success, supplied witness or board-correspondence premise occurs in
this bound. Its honest-rejection term is an actual event probability, not a
proved negligible allowance.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{ElectionDDHExtractedFinish}{ElectionDDHSource.extracted_finish_distance_le}

The proof charges honest rejection and accepted extraction failure, then uses
the exact retained-original distribution.
This distributional composition does
not replace the separately checked source-position or extraction proofs.
On
bad branches the finisher still publishes the original prefix, submission,
decision, board and encrypted tally.
Its shares follow the explicit fallback
formula; they need not be correct there.
\companchor{ElectionDDHExtractedFinish}{ElectionDDHSource.finishExtracted_publication}

\paragraph{Controls and composition boundary.}
At $q=257$, $pk=3$, $K=(5,7)$ and malicious nonces $(6,9)$ give accepted shares
$(33,48)$ and rejected shares $(15,21)$.
Missing extraction differs from the
correct accepted share.
A rejected honest ballot gives a counterexample to
using the pair sum without both honest acceptances.
An actual querying non-DH
prefix and its original guessing callback instantiate the complete bound with
$N=10$ and context allowance $33\delta$.
\companchor{ElectionDDHExtractedFinishControls}{ElectionDDHExtractedFinishControls.accepted_shares}
\companchor{ElectionDDHExtractedFinishControls}{ElectionDDHExtractedFinishControls.rejected_shares}
\companchor{ElectionDDHExtractedFinishControls}{ElectionDDHExtractedFinishControls.missing_witness_differs}
\companchor{ElectionDDHExtractedFinishControls}{ElectionDDHExtractedFinishControls.rejected_honest_sum_differs}
\companchor{ElectionDDHExtractedFinishControls}{ElectionDDHExtractedFinishControls.finish_after_queries}

Independent multiplicative subgroup fixtures check 7,986 finishing columns,
including malicious rejection, arbitrary challenges and missing-witness and
honest-rejection counterfactuals.
The extended pairing fixture detects nine
mutants with zero failures/discards; its arithmetic decision histories do not
claim reachable protocol execution.
The separate 1,800-case honest-source
fixture retains actual decision/cache checks and seven detected mutants.

The following cost theorem derives the complete source budget.
The later components bound $R$ in both challenge worlds and choose $\delta,k$ with explicit advantage and polynomial trial counts.

\subsection{Historical component: Complete source interaction costs}\label{sec:source-cost}
The cost counts every uniform-index oracle request, including private proof
coins and auxiliary hash misses.
It counts a cached auxiliary answer as zero new
draws.
Pure computation, operand widths and bit-operation costs are outside this
metric and remain necessary for the standard-PPT theorem.

\begin{theorem}[Actual extracted-game interaction bound, machine-checked]\label{thm:source-cost}
Let $F$ be a finite field with decidable equality and a sampler using at most one
uniform-index query, and $G$ an additive $F$-module with decidable equality.
Fix arbitrary $g,pk,A,T\in G$, preparation and the original adversary callbacks.
Suppose preparation uses at most $P$ total requests, every casting callback at
most $C$, and every guessing callback at most $D$. Then the actual prefix has
complete interaction bound
\[
 M=4P+4C+78.
\]
For every selector bound $n$ and repetition count $k$, the implemented extracted
game, including uniform replay interpretation and final publication/guessing,
has total interaction bound
\[
 (1+3k)M+4+4D.
\]
This theorem covers every answer branch and every rejection or extraction
fallback. It assumes no complete-source cost or acceptance certificate.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{ElectionDDHSourceCost}{ElectionDDHSource.prefix_total_bound}
\companchor{ElectionDDHSourceCost}{ElectionDDHSource.extractedGame_total_bound}

The proof derives the full-election lower-handler cost from its actual cases:
one forwarded request or one scalar draw on an auxiliary miss, and no draw on
an auxiliary hit.
The existing ballot programmer costs at most four oracle
nodes per source request.
Its statement simulator uses four scalar draws even
when its programming target collides.
These conservative bounds yield the
following prefix account:
\[
 \underbrace{4P}_{\rm preparation}+\underbrace{4}_{\rm vote}
 +\underbrace{2}_{\rm key\ proof}+\underbrace{12}_{\rm known\ nonces}
 +\underbrace{48}_{\rm honest\ ballots/submissions}
 +\underbrace{4C}_{\rm casting}+\underbrace{12}_{\rm submission}.
\]
\companchor{RepairedSubmissionCost}{ballotSim_total_query_bound}
\companchor{ElectionDDHSourceCost}{ElectionDDHSource.lower_total_bound}

Final trustee simulation uses four scalar draws, and guessing contributes the
conservative $4D$ from arbitrary incoming state/live cache.
The existing
repetition theorem supplies the factor $1+3k$, with its formerly assumed source
budget now derived.
Interpreting the actual wrapped replay oracle with the
uniform sampler preserves this cost bound.
\companchor{ElectionDDHSourceCost}{ElectionDDHSource.finish_total_bound}

For prime $q$, the pinned default sampler on $\mathbb Z/q\mathbb Z$ uses one
uniform finite-index request.
Its existing theorem instantiates the scalar cost
premise, so the prime-field complete-game result takes only the stated callback
query bounds.
It still requires a separate bit-level cost correspondence.
\companchor{ElectionDDHSourceCost}{ElectionDDHSource.extractedGame_prime_total_bound}

\paragraph{Controls and component scope.}
The actual querying callbacks over $q=257$ have $P=2,C=1,D=1$, yielding prefix
bound $90$ and complete bound $(1+3k)90+8$.
Controls distinguish an auxiliary
miss from a hit despite both having zero live ballot hashes, and show that a
discarded private draw still counts as an interaction.
\companchor{ElectionDDHSourceCostControls}{ElectionDDHSourceCostControls.prefix_after_queries}
\companchor{ElectionDDHSourceCostControls}{ElectionDDHSourceCostControls.complete_after_queries}
\companchor{ElectionDDHSourceCostControls}{ElectionDDHSourceCostControls.auxiliary_miss_cost}
\companchor{ElectionDDHSourceCostControls}{ElectionDDHSourceCostControls.auxiliary_hit_cost}
\companchor{ElectionDDHSourceCostControls}{ElectionDDHSourceCostControls.discarded_draw_cost}

Independent upper-account enumeration checks 78,732 DDH cases and detects five
omitted-cost rules, with zero failures/discards.
Its branch lengths are accounting
bounds, not assertions of reachable protocol histories or exact runtime.
The
existing submission/replay cost controls remain.
The following public test bounds honest rejection in both DDH worlds.
Extraction parameter choices, the complete advantage inequality and standard-PPT
adequacy are outside this component's guarantee.
This interaction count alone does not make arbitrary host
callbacks polynomial-time attackers.

\subsection{Historical component: Public rejection in the DDH games}\label{sec:ddh-rejection}
The public rejection test receives $(g,pk,A,T)$, executes the actual prepared
prefix and returns whether its two honest decisions are not both accepting.
It includes preparation queries and the malicious casting/submission, and uses
neither a secret key nor extraction.
It fits the pinned VCVio
\texttt{DiffieHellman.DDHAdversary} interface directly.

The real DDH game draws $a,b\gets U(F)$ and supplies
$(g,ag,bg,(ab)g)$; the random game also draws independent $c\gets U(F)$ and
supplies $(g,ag,bg,cg)$.
Write $R_{\rm real},R_{\rm rand}$ for this test's
output-true probabilities.
Its two-game advantage is
\[
 \Delta_R=\left|\operatorname{toReal}(R_{\rm real})-
                    \operatorname{toReal}(R_{\rm rand})\right|.
\]
This is twice the corresponding guessing advantage.
All exponent sampling is
from the full field, as in the pinned DDH games.

\begin{theorem}[Actual DDH honest-rejection bounds, machine-checked]\label{thm:ddh-rejection}
Let $F$ be a finite field with its sampler and decidable equality, and $G$ an
additive $F$-module with decidable equality. Suppose $r\mapsto rg$ is injective.
Let preparation have at most $p$ proof-hash requests and every casting callback
at most $c$, counting all proof domains. Put $q=|F|$ and
\[
 \rho=\frac{9}{q-1}+\operatorname{ofReal}
                   \left(\frac{11p+2c+131}{q}\right).
\]
Then, in $\mathbb R_{\ge0}^{\infty}$,
\[
 R_{\rm real}\le\rho,
 \qquad R_{\rm rand}\le R_{\rm real}+\operatorname{ofReal}(\Delta_R)
                    \le\rho+\operatorname{ofReal}(\Delta_R).
\]
The first probability equals the actual honest-rejection event in the complete
real-challenge comparison, including its later guessing execution. These
inequalities require no acceptance, supplied freshness, negligible-rejection or
DDH-hardness premise. The actual test's distinguishing advantage remains explicit.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{ElectionDDHRejection}{ElectionDDHSource.honestReject_real}
\companchor{ElectionDDHRejection}{ElectionDDHSource.honestReject_real_le}
\companchor{ElectionDDHRejection}{ElectionDDHSource.honestReject_random_transfer}
\companchor{ElectionDDHRejection}{ElectionDDHSource.honestReject_random_le}

The real bound composes the original election's nonce-collision rejection
allowance with the complete $131/q$-constant comparison.
The real-game identity
uses the supported finishing theorem: later proof generation and guessing
preserve the original public honest decisions.
The random bound uses the
absolute two-game probability difference; it does not identify a random tuple
with a real one or resample rejected executions.
\companchor{ElectionProgrammedExtraction}{ElectionProgrammedExtraction.original_prepared_rejection_le}

If the scalar sampler costs at most one uniform-index request, and complete
preparation/casting request bounds are $P,C$, the public test has interaction
bound $4P+4C+78$.
This derives live-interpreter and prefix costs and does not
execute the guessing callback.
Bit-operation costs remain separate.
\companchor{ElectionDDHSourceCost}{ElectionDDHSource.ballot_runtime_total_bound}
\companchor{ElectionDDHRejection}{ElectionDDHSource.honestReject_total_bound}

\paragraph{Controls and composition boundary.}
The actual querying instance over $q=257$ has real allowance
$9/256+155/257<1$, random allowance equal to that plus its actual DDH advantage,
and interaction bound $90$.
Changing the guessing suffix leaves this public
test unchanged.
An independent two-world table with rejection masses $0,1$
refutes omitting the advantage or substituting half of it.

This validates probability

\subsection{Historical component: Effective integer extraction parameters}\label{sec:replay-parameters}
\paragraph{Construction and scope.}
For live prefix hash budget $N$, field cardinality $q$ and a positive natural
accuracy request $e$, define
\[
 \delta=\frac{1}{6(N+1)e},\qquad
 k=3456(N+1)^3e^4.
\]
The implemented extractor computes only natural $N,k$; $\delta$ is an analysis
threshold.
The same fixed original execution is retained on every outcome.

\begin{theorem}[Extraction accuracy with an executable repetition count]
For $N,q,e\in\mathbb N$, $e>0$ and $12(N+1)e\le q$, extended-real truncated
subtraction gives
\[
 3(N+1)\delta+\bigl(1-(\delta-q^{-1})^3\bigr)^k\le e^{-1}.
\]
\end{theorem}
\companchor{BallotReplayParameters}{ballotReplay_parameters}
\begin{proof}[Checked proof structure]
The context loss is $1/(2e)$.
The field-size condition implies
$t=(\delta-q^{-1})^3\ge 1/(1728(N+1)^3e^3)$, so $kt\ge 2e$.
For $0\le t\le1$, induction proves
$(1-t)^k(1+kt)\le1$, hence $(1-t)^k\le(1+kt)^{-1}\le(2e)^{-1}$.
Positivity and the transfer to extended-real subtraction are checked explicitly.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Actual full-election instance.}
Set $N=p+c+9$, where $p,c$ bound live ballot hashes of preparation and every
casting callback.
For nonzero $g$, secret $s$, arbitrary public challenge
coordinates $A,T$ and the same callbacks as Section~\ref{sec:extracted-finish},
let $X_k$ be the actual public-input extracted game at $\mathit{pk}=sg$ and
$Y_s$ the actual secret-using completed comparison source.
If
$e>0$ and $12(p+c+10)e\le |F|$, then
\[
 \operatorname{TV}(X_k,Y_s)
 \le \Pr[\text{honest pair rejected in the original prefix}]+e^{-1}.
\]
The comparison retains the complete public result, winning bit, private source
state and live cache.
The rejection term is an actual event, whose real/random
DDH bounds are established above.
No extraction-success, accepted-board or
external witness premise is supplied.
\companchor{ElectionDDHParameters}{ElectionDDHSource.extracted_finish_accuracy}

For the pinned prime-field sampler, complete callback interaction bounds
$P,C,D$ give that same game's uniform-index query bound
\[
 \bigl(1+3\cdot3456(N+1)^3e^4\bigr)(4P+4C+78)+4+4D.
\]
This cost holds for every public $(g,\mathit{pk},A,T)$ and natural $N,e$;
the size and positivity conditions are needed for accuracy.
Pure operations
and standard-PPT execution are still outside this bound.
\companchor{ElectionDDHParameters}{ElectionDDHSource.extractedGame_accuracy_cost}

\paragraph{Controls and component scope.}
At $N=0,e=2,q=29$, $k=55{,}296$ gives the non-vacuous allowance $1/2$.
At $q=2$, the same threshold has zero truncated success and residual failure
one: removing the size condition is false.
Zero trials also fail; $e=0$ gives
an infinite allowance and is vacuous.
A complete $q=257$, $N=9$, $e=2$ game
instantiates the bound without evaluating its $55{,}296{,}000$-attempt tree.
A separate querying callback fixture retains all original/replay/guess costs.
\companchor{BallotReplayParametersControls}{BallotReplayParametersControls.positive_accuracy}
\companchor{BallotReplayParametersControls}{BallotReplayParametersControls.field_size_required}
\companchor{BallotReplayParametersControls}{BallotReplayParametersControls.zero_accuracy_vacuous}
\companchor{BallotReplayParametersControls}{BallotReplayParametersControls.zero_trials_fail}
\companchor{BallotReplayParametersControls}{BallotReplayParametersControls.complete_accuracy}
\companchor{BallotReplayParametersControls}{BallotReplayParametersControls.querying_cost}

Independent rational enumeration checks 408 parameter cases and 4,225 geometric
cases, detecting omitted field size, linear repetition and a missing joint cube;
the existing 1,800 fixed-original replay cases remain.
These finite tests have
zero failures/discards.
Section~\ref{sec:random-source} proves complete random-mask secrecy for the
comparison source.
The parameterized theorem in the formal reference supplies its field-size and polynomial-accuracy conditions. Choosing negligible $\delta$ alone does not establish polynomial repetition.


\subsection{Historical component: Random-mask secrecy of the complete comparison}\label{sec:random-source}
\paragraph{Comparison and boundary.}
The actual \code{completedReal} source first runs preparation, samples the
hidden vote, programs the key and both honest ballot proofs, performs honest
and malicious submissions, publishes trustee shares/proofs and calls the
saved-state-dependent guesser.
In this mathematical hybrid, a secret $s$
computes shares of the retained board.
The executable DDH reduction must use
the separately checked public-input extractor and finisher.

\begin{theorem}[Complete random-mask comparison]
Let $F$ be a sampleable field, $G$ a decidable additive commutative group with
an $F$-module structure, and let $F$ have decidable equality. Fix any public
$g,\mathit{pk},A\in G$, mathematical secret $s\in F$, fingerprint function,
preparation computation and family of casting/guessing callbacks with the
original public interface. For any fixed source state $S$ and live cache $L$,
\[
 \Pr\left[\mathsf{win}\ \middle|\
   z\gets U(F);\
   \mathsf{evaluate}\bigl(\mathsf{completedReal}
      (g,\mathit{pk},A,zg,s),S,L\bigr)\right]=\frac12.
\]
The displayed arguments suppress only the fixed fingerprint, preparation and
callback family. No acceptance, freshness, board-shape, query-budget or cache
consistency premise is required for this comparison result.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{ElectionDDHRandomSource}{ElectionDDHSource.completed_random_win}

\begin{proof}[Checked source and probability argument]
A private unscored continuation performs the same casting, submission,
publication and guessing, returning the actual guess.
An exact runtime identity
recovers the original completed source by scoring that guess against the vote,
retaining the full source state and live cache.
For a fixed vote $v$, the
bijection $z\mapsto z-\mathsf{voteScalar}(v)$ preserves the uniform mask law.
The paired ciphertext identity then gives equality of the entire unscored
continuation, including its oracle requests and rejection paths.
Independent
mask and vote draws commute only in interpreted distributions.
The resulting
unscored output is independent of the fair vote, so the actual guess wins with
probability $1/2$.
\end{proof}

This proof does not infer replay-tree equality from semantic equality.
It
reasons about the actual non-replayed comparison, whose full-output distance
from the extractor-based game was proved separately.
Using this result in DDH requires charging that distance and the random-world rejection allowance before applying the exact hybrid equality.

\paragraph{Controls and finite validation.}
Over field $257$, checked instances retain querying preparation/casting/guessing,
a nonempty initial cache and source record/flag, and a further callback whose
saved state depends on board length and whose later query and guess depend on
published decryption shares.
The paired ciphertexts distinguish the two
fixed-zero-mask inputs; this is a masking control, not a complete attack bound.
Uniform masks equate both input laws, and handing the hidden vote directly to
a guesser makes its success one.
\companchor{ElectionDDHRandomSourceControls}{ElectionDDHRandomSourceControls.querying_complete}
\companchor{ElectionDDHRandomSourceControls}{ElectionDDHRandomSourceControls.prior_cache_complete}
\companchor{ElectionDDHRandomSourceControls}{ElectionDDHRandomSourceControls.view_dependent_complete}
\companchor{ElectionDDHRandomSourceControls}{ElectionDDHRandomSourceControls.fixed_mask_ciphertext_leaks}
\companchor{ElectionDDHRandomSourceControls}{ElectionDDHRandomSourceControls.uniform_mask_both_votes}
\companchor{ElectionDDHRandomSourceControls}{ElectionDDHRandomSourceControls.leaked_vote_wins}

The independent multiplicative fixture checks 660 mask-coupled executions on
fixed tapes, extending its existing 1,800 honest-source cases.
Selected replay
and corruption callbacks query retained oracle answers, publish retained-board
shares and trustee proofs, and guess from the view and saved answers.
All three
honest decisions occur; coupled full fixture records agree, and each enumerated
uniform-mask table has winning mass $1/2$.
Fixed-mask ciphertext leakage is
detected.
These selected continuations validate finite arithmetic and transcript
behavior; the Lean theorem supplies universal callback quantification.

Section~\ref{sec:finite-ddh-reduction} supplies the executable public-input DDH
distinguisher and complete finite advantage inequality, including both worlds'
comparison losses and the honest-rejection test's DDH advantage.


\subsection{Historical component: Finite reduction of the original prepared election}\label{sec:finite-ddh-reduction}
\paragraph{Actual algorithms.}
Let $N=p+c+9$ and $k=3456(N+1)^3e^4$.
The main DDH distinguisher $D_{N,e}$
receives only $(g,\mathit{pk},A,T)$, runs the actual \code{extractedGame} with
these inputs and natural $N,k$, and returns its winning bit.
The second test
$D_{\rm rej}$ executes the original prepared prefix and reports honest-pair
rejection.
Both algorithms close over the original fingerprint and attacker
callbacks.
Neither receives a secret, an ideal transcript or an extraction
success certificate.
Extraction failures and rejected boards are retained.

\begin{theorem}[Complete finite DDH advantage bound]
In the original fixed three-voter, two-candidate, one-honest-trustee scope, let
$F$ be a finite sampleable field with decidable equality, $q=|F|$, and let $G$
be a decidable additive commutative group with an $F$-module structure. Assume
$r\mapsto rg$ is injective. Let preparation use at most $p$ proof-hash queries,
and every casting callback at most $c$, counting all three proof domains.
The saved-state-dependent guessing callback is arbitrary. For natural $e>0$
with $12(p+c+10)e\le q$, define
\[
 L=\frac{11p+2c+131}{q},\qquad
 \Delta_g(D)=\left|\Pr[D(\mathsf{DDH}_{\rm real})=1]
                    -\Pr[D(\mathsf{DDH}_{\rm random})=1]\right|.
\]
Then the actual shared-oracle prepared election satisfies
\[
 \left|\Pr[\mathsf{win}]-\frac12\right|
 \le \frac{18}{q-1}+3L+\frac2e
      +\Delta_g(D_{N,e})+\Delta_g(D_{\rm rej}).
\]
\end{theorem}
\companchor{ElectionDDHReduction}{ElectionDDHSource.prepared_ballot_secrecy_ddh_bound}
The source theorem expresses $1/(q-1)$ through \code{noncePointBound} and
\code{toReal}.
It runs the original prepared election through the existing
shared-cache interpreter from an empty cache.
The original nonzero key and
honest nonce sampling are preserved.
The actual public transcript, rejection,
trustee output, prepared state and later guessing stay in the game.

\paragraph{Every loss is accounted for.}
Put $\rho=9/(q-1)+L$.
The checked chain is:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule
Comparison & Allowance\\
\midrule
Original election to real secret-using comparison & $L$\\
Real comparison to actual real DDH test & $R_{\rm real}+1/e$\\
Real to random actual DDH test & $\Delta_g(D_{N,e})$\\
Actual random test to random secret-using comparison & $R_{\rm random}+1/e$\\
Random comparison to fair guessing & $0$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Here $R_{\rm real}\le\rho$ and
$R_{\rm random}\le\rho+\Delta_g(D_{\rm rej})$ are proved actual-event bounds.
The real/random extraction comparisons are averaged over the pinned DDH
experiments' sampled coordinates, using both directions of the event-probability
bound.
The original winning projection is derived from
\code{preparedSource\_project}; no caller supplies a correspondence premise.
\companchor{ElectionDDHReduction}{ElectionDDHSource.ballotSecrecy_real_gap}
\companchor{ElectionDDHReduction}{ElectionDDHSource.ballotSecrecy_random_gap}

\paragraph{Interaction cost and remaining efficiency boundary.}
For the pinned prime-field sampler, all-request preparation/casting/guessing
bounds $P,C,D$ give the actual main distinguisher cost
\[
 \bigl(1+3\cdot3456(N+1)^3e^4\bigr)(4P+4C+78)+4+4D.
\]
These are uniform-index oracle nodes.
Live ballot queries are bounded by all
proof hashes, so the final proof derives its live $p,c$ premises from the
stated all-proof-hash bounds.
Complete callback budgets $P,C,D$ separately
charge randomness and all proof domains.
The rejection test's corresponding
cost is already $4P+4C+78$.
\companchor{ElectionDDHReduction}{ElectionDDHSource.ballotSecrecy_prime_cost}

\paragraph{Controls and status.}
The $q=65{,}537$, $p=2$, $c=1$, $e=16$ instance retains querying preparation,
board-dependent casting state and share-dependent querying/guessing.
Its
explicit non-DDH allowance $18/65{,}536+465/65{,}537+1/8$ is below $1/2$.
This is a finite test field, with no asserted DDH hardness.
Its actual reduction
has the matching checked query bound.
Independent probability controls require
both extraction allowances, the original loss, absolute values and the two-game
DDH normalization; a zero generator fails the injectivity premise.

\companchor{ElectionDDHReductionControls}{ElectionDDHReductionControls.allowance_nonvacuous}

Independent exact arithmetic checks 19,683 probability chains and detects seven
omitted-loss, signed-advantage and factor-two mutants with no failures/discards.
These tables validate the reduction arithmetic; the Lean theorem establishes

The formal reference gives the parameterized theorem, its attacker family and protocol scope. It retains explicit external reduction-efficiency assumptions.

\subsection{Historical component: Known repair control under the shared oracle}\label{sec:oracle-repair-control}
The repaired attack issues the full neutral-ciphertext proof query
\[
 H_{\mathrm{ballot}}\bigl(g,pk,(0,0),((g,pk),(g,pk+g))\bigr)=c.
\]
It constructs the neutral proof with challenges $c-1,1$ and responses $1,1$,
then copies the first accepted ballot's aggregate ciphertext and aggregate
proof into the original malicious submission.
The ordinary prefix and finisher
execute with the same cache, including all key, ballot and trustee proof queries.
\companchor{ElectionRepairControl}{ElectionOracle.repairedReuseSubmission_function}
\companchor{ElectionRepairControl}{ElectionOracle.repairedAttackWithCoins_function}
\companchor{ElectionRepairControl}{ElectionOracle.repairedAttackWorld_function}

\begin{theorem}[Known repaired attack: oracle-aware rejection]
Let $F$ be a finite field with decidable equality and the stated uniform sampling
instance, and let $G$ be an additive commutative $F$-module with decidable equality.
For every generator $g$, fingerprint function, vote $v$ and initial shared cache
$Q$, the original nonzero-sampled repaired attack satisfies
\[
 \Pr\bigl[\mathit{view.decision}=\mathsf{reusedCiphertext}
   \mid \mathsf{run}(\mathsf{repairedAttackWorld}(g,v),Q)\bigr]=1.
\]
The same result holds for every fixed secret, key/trustee nonce tuple and honest
coin tuple, without a collision-free or honest-pair-acceptance premise.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{ElectionRepairControl}{ElectionOracle.repairedAttackWorld_rejected}
\companchor{ElectionRepairControl}{ElectionOracle.repairedAttackWithCoins_rejected}

The fixed-hash interpretation equals the original complete execution and sampled
world.
This equality retains the public prefix, submission, rejection reason,
board, encrypted tally, decryption shares, trustee proofs and bounded decoding.
The proof instantiates pinned VCVio's probability-one characterization for a
mixed uniform/lazy oracle: every consistent deterministic answer table satisfies
the existing repair control while uniform draws remain probabilistic.
This
argument supplies no general quantitative transfer for the unrepaired attack.

\paragraph{Controls and boundary.}
Six Lean controls check actual cache hits/misses, the literal neutral transcript,
sampled rejection, invalid-proof priority and honest acceptance with the existing
component-only-weeding counterfactual.
Independent multiplicative fixtures check
240 executions (seeds 17, 41, 103), including both votes and both cache cases.
Both honest acceptance and rejection occur; all component-only mutants accept
and all changed-response controls fail verification before weeding.
The fixtures
check the retained board, decoded tally and all trustee equations, with zero
failures or discarded cases.
They do not prove a distributional correspondence
for arbitrary implementations.

\companchor{ElectionRepairControlControls}{ElectionRepairControlControls.neutral_miss}

\companchor{ElectionRepairControlControls}{ElectionRepairControlControls.sampled_rejection}

\subsection{Historical component: Fixed finite-control oracle interface}\label{sec:bit-oracle-interface}
The deferred fully mechanized efficiency approach requires executable local control as well as query bounds.
This interface reuses Mathlib's TM2 stack instructions, with fixed
finite stack, label and local-memory types.
Unbounded words reside on stacks.
A code object is fixed for the whole parameter family.
Explicit hash and coin
instructions suspend with a named response destination; resumption replaces that
stack and retains every other stack.
All-local runs preserve the original TM2
configuration exactly.
\companchor{BitOracleMachine}{BitOracleMachine.resume_frame}
\companchor{BitOracleMachine}{BitOracleMachine.local_step}
\companchor{BitOracleMachine}{BitOracleMachine.local_run}

A raw hash instruction carries the ghost charge
$1+|\mathit{request}|+|\mathit{oldDestination}|+|\mathit{answer}|$;
a coin replacement carries $2+|\mathit{oldDestination}|$.
These are the proposed port-transfer accounting rules.
The cost field is proof
instrumentation, not an implemented counter or a standard-machine cost theorem.
The existing deterministic TM2-to-TM1 simulation does not yet implement these
open-oracle transfers or give their quantitative bound.

\begin{theorem}[Adaptive raw-port execution test]
One fixed program with four labels, four stacks and one local-memory value
executes the following exact query tree for every input word $x$:
\[
 a\leftarrow H(x);\quad b\leftarrow H(a);\quad c\leftarrow H(x).
\]
After four steps it halts with stacks $[x,a,b,c]$ and derived interface charge
\[
 4+2|x|+2|a|+|b|+|c|.
\]
Equality holds as an OracleComp program, so every handler observes the same
queries, responses and final configuration.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{BitOracleCanary}{BitOracleCanary.run_source}

Five Lean controls check a literal miss/miss/hit run with charge 15, an occupied
first entry that changes the second query and gives charge 11, observable fuel
exhaustion, coin replacement and omitted transfer charges.
Independent fixtures
cover all binary words of lengths 0--6, four deterministic response functions
and three initial-cache choices: 1,524 cases, plus 130 coin replacements.
They
detect forgetting the original request and dropping request/answer charges,
with zero failures or discarded cases.

\subsection{Historical component: Actual oracle-port word movement}\label{sec:port-transfer}
The raw-port interface needs a physical request copy and response replacement.
The existing copy routine preserves its source but prepends to the destination;
a nonempty destination therefore exposes a checked mismatch with replacement.
The transfer first clears that destination and reuses the checked copy program.
For responses it also erases the private source after copying.
Otherwise the
next request inherits an uncharged old private word.
A checked stale-port control
retains this insufficient workspace invariant as a failed approach.
\companchor{BitPortTransfer}{BitPortTransfer.append_is_not_replacement}
\companchor{BitPortTransferControls}{BitPortTransferControls.uncleared_port_not_free}

The source-preserving routine takes at most $|d|+2|w|+4$ TM2 ticks; the consuming
routine takes at most $|d|+3|w|+4$.
Both bounds include end and return checks.
Existing stack-placement and return libraries are reused.
A product-memory
frame preserves the caller's finite local memory at identical transition counts.
The actual caller layout, incoming word, empty scratch and other stacks are
derived by the adapter, rather than supplied as correspondence premises.
\companchor{BitPortTransfer}{BitPortTransfer.run}
\companchor{BitPortTransfer}{BitPortTransfer.consume_run}
\companchor{TM2MemoryFrame}{TM2MemoryFrame.statement}
\companchor{TM2MemoryFrame}{TM2MemoryFrame.tick}
\companchor{TM2MemoryFrame}{TM2MemoryFrame.run}

\begin{theorem}[Physical port transfer for the raw interface]
For every caller configuration, selected request/destination ports and saved
return address, let $r$ be the request word and $d$ the old destination.
Starting with empty private ports, there is a request-phase execution of at most
$2|r|+4$ TM2 ticks that places exactly $r$ on the query port. For every subsequent native answer
$a$, the response phase halts at the exact specified \code{resume} state, retaining
all other caller stacks and finite memory and restoring empty private ports.
The total number of TM2 ticks plus the one native event is at most
\[
 9(1+|r|+|d|+|a|).
\]
A native coin answer has the same successor/empty-workspace guarantee and bound
$9(2+|d|)$. Request and destination may be the same caller port.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{BitOraclePortTransfer}{BitOraclePortTransfer.hash_transfer}
\companchor{BitOraclePortTransfer}{BitOraclePortTransfer.coin_transfer}

The native hash/coin operations are actual OracleComp queries.
Their handoff
identifies the prepared query word and the complete response-phase start under
every handler, including the saved caller state.

\companchor{BitOraclePortTransfer}{BitOraclePortTransfer.coin_handoff}

hash cases and 126 coin cases.
Append, one-pass and source-erasure mutants are

\subsection{Historical component: Finite source execution in the assembled loop}\label{sec:oracle-loop}
The loop has three control forms: caller-ready, request preparation and response
loading.
Its unbounded data are bit stacks; saved addresses, port indices and
local memory are finite.
A transition executes one existing TM2 statement,
selects a phase, issues a native query, or returns to the caller.
It contains no
residual OracleComp program and computes no word length to select execution
fuel.
Fuel belongs to external observation.

Request preparation stops at its first completed state before issuing a native
query.
Response loading derives empty private I/O and scratch before discarding
those ports at return.
This prevents padded halted subroutine ticks from being
mistaken for loop ticks that would issue the next query.
Dispatch, native events
and returns increase the transfer bound to $11(1+|r|+|d|+|a|)$ for a hash and
$11(2+|d|)$ for a coin.
Request and destination may alias.
\companchor{BitOracleLoop}{BitOracleLoop.request_run}
\companchor{BitOracleLoop}{BitOracleLoop.response_run}
\companchor{BitOracleLoop}{BitOracleLoop.hash_issue}
\companchor{BitOracleLoop}{BitOracleLoop.hash_cycle}
\companchor{BitOracleLoop}{BitOracleLoop.coin_cycle}
\companchor{BitOracleLoop}{BitOracleLoop.run_add}
\companchor{BitOraclePortTransfer}{BitOraclePortTransfer.delivery_start}

For fixed code, write $U_t(s)$ for its actual $t$-step loop observation and
$\mathsf{ready}(c)$ for a caller boundary.
The proof predicate
$R(Q,p,s)$ (\code{Realizes}) relates a source query tree $Q$, already spent
steps $p$ and loop state $s$.
It is defined by structural recursion on $Q$:
\[
\begin{aligned}
R(\mathsf{pure}(c,C),p,s)
&\iff \exists t,\ p+t\leq 11C\ \land\
 U_t(s)=\mathsf{pure}(\mathsf{ready}(c)),\\
R(q\mathbin{\triangleright}k,p,s)
&\iff \exists t,\delta,\ U_t(s)=q\mathbin{\triangleright}(\mathsf{pure}\circ\delta)
 \\[-1mm]
&\hspace{14mm}\land\ \forall a,\ R(k(a),p+t,\delta(a)).
\end{aligned}
\]
Here $q\mathbin{\triangleright}k$ is one actual typed native query with
continuation $k$.
Equality at each query boundary preserves every answer branch.
The witness is constructed in the proof; it is not an executable field or a
caller-supplied adequacy assumption.

\begin{theorem}[Costed finite raw-source execution]
For every fixed raw code $P$, caller configuration $c$ and finite source
observation fuel $n$,
\[
 R(\mathsf{sourceRun}(P,n,c),0,\mathsf{ready}(c)).
\]
For every typed path through that source run, ending in $(c',C)$, there are
$t\leq11C$ and a typed path through $U_t(\mathsf{ready}(c))$ with output
$\mathsf{ready}(c')$ and exactly the same complete query/answer log.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{BitOracleLoopCorrespondence}{BitOracleLoop.run_source}
\companchor{BitOracleLoopCorrespondence}{BitOracleLoop.realizes_path}
\companchor{BitOracleLoopCorrespondence}{BitOracleLoop.run_source_path}

The path statement reuses pinned PolyFun paths and VCVio query logs.
It assumes
no raw-response length bound: observation times may depend on answers already
received.
It is not a uniform-fuel probability-law theorem or a standard oracle-
tape cost theorem.
The next subsection supplies common-clock handler transport
under explicit termination and answer bounds.

Six Lean controls include the all-word adaptive canary, literal 63-step
miss/miss/hit and 52-step occupied-cache executions, the silent preparation/native
event boundary, and rejection of a zero-charge witness for a changed caller.
Independent fixtures compare 2,880 three-call executions: all 64 sequences from
three hash-port layouts and a coin, input lengths 0--3, response lengths 0--4,
and seeds 7, 19 and 41.
Early-query, stale-private-word and skipped-successor
mutants are detected.
Failures and discarded cases are zero.

\subsection{Historical component: Common-clock execution and handler transport}\label{sec:oracle-bounded}
A uniform clock requires an encoded native-answer bound.
The bounded interface
retains every raw request and both coin values, admitting hash answers of length
at most $L$.
Its adapter erases only the bound proof.
The actual scalar handler
must still derive this contract.

Write $A_L(Q)$ for interpretation through this adapter.
$\mathsf{Within}_{L,B}(Q)$ requires every allowed-answer branch to end in $(c,C)$
with $c.\mathsf{label}=\mathsf{none}$ and $C\leq B$, recursively at queries.
It asserts source termination and accounting, not correspondence.

\begin{theorem}[Common-clock source and handler correspondence]
Let $P$ be any fixed raw code, $c$ any caller configuration and $n$ a finite source
observation fuel. If
$\mathsf{Within}_{L,B}(\mathsf{sourceRun}(P,n,c))$, then
\[
 A_L(U_{11B}(\mathsf{ready}(c)))
 = (\mathsf{ready}\circ\mathsf{fst})\mathbin{\langle\$\rangle}
     A_L(\mathsf{sourceRun}(P,n,c)).
\]
The equality holds for the complete query trees and is preserved by interpretation
through every lawful handler of the bounded interface, including stateful
probabilistic handlers. The complete caller state and handler effects are
retained; only the source's ghost cost is projected away.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{BitOracleLoopBounded}{BitOracleLoopBounded.realizes_run}
\companchor{BitOracleLoopBounded}{BitOracleLoopBounded.run_source}
\companchor{BitOracleLoopBounded}{BitOracleLoopBounded.run_source_handler}

The source theorem constructs the execution witness; halted leaves absorb
remaining ticks.
Termination is necessary: the checked spinning-coin program
makes one query in one source instruction but three in 22 loop ticks.
The termination predicate rejects this padding counterexample.
\companchor{BitOracleBoundedControls}{BitOracleBoundedControls.missing_halt_changes_trace}
\companchor{BitOracleBoundedControls}{BitOracleBoundedControls.spinning_not_within}

For the existing adaptive $H(w),H(H(w)),H(w)$ canary, the actual source halt and
bound $B=4+2|w|+4L$ are derived.
Its loop and arbitrary-handler equalities therefore
need no caller-supplied cost, termination or correspondence premise.
The common
clock is $11(4+2|w|+4L)$.
\companchor{BitOracleBoundedCanary}{BitOracleBoundedCanary.within}
\companchor{BitOracleBoundedCanary}{BitOracleBoundedCanary.run}
\companchor{BitOracleBoundedCanary}{BitOracleBoundedCanary.run_handler}

Seven Lean controls cover cache effects, zero-width answers, intact requests,
missing termination and oversized answers.
Independent fixtures check 2,880
three-call cases at 528 and 1056 ticks, retaining complete tapes/cache/trace,
halt and private workspace.
Input lengths are 0--3, response limit 4, source
bound 48, seeds 7/19/41.
Missing-halt and early-fuel mutants are detected;
failures and discarded cases are zero.

\subsection{Historical component: Quantitative reuse of the stack-to-tape translation}\label{sec:tm2-tape-cost}
Mathlib's existing compiler represents stacks as reversed columns on one tape,
with a bottom marker.
Each stack operation scans outward to the selected top,
performs the operation, and returns.
Its correctness theorem erases execution
length.
The new proofs reuse that compiler and its complete \code{TrCfg}
relation, adding counts rather than a second encoding.

An outward scan of $n$ cells takes $n$ TM1 ticks; return from position $n'$
takes $n'$ ticks.
The existing top-action correctness theorem preserves all
other columns.
Including the operation and continuation dispatch gives
$n+n'+2$ ticks for one stack access.
\companchor{TM2TapeCost}{TM2TapeCost.scan_right}
\companchor{TM2TapeCost}{TM2TapeCost.scan_left}
\companchor{TM2TapeCost}{TM2TapeCost.top_action}
\companchor{TM2TapeCost}{TM2TapeCost.roundtrip}

\begin{theorem}[Complete translated-statement cost]
Let $(\mathsf{some}(\ell),v,S)$ be a source configuration, $c$ a related tape
configuration under Mathlib's \code{TrCfg}, and $|S_k|\leq H$ for every stack.
Let $a$ be the maximum number of stack accesses along a branch of $M(\ell)$.
There are $t$ and $c'$ such that
\[
 t\leq 1+a(2(H+a)+2),\qquad
 (\mathsf{tapeTick}_M)^t(c)=c',
\]
and $c'$ is related to $\mathsf{stepAux}(M(\ell),v,S)$ by \code{TrCfg}.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{TM2TapeCost}{TM2TapeCost.statement}
\companchor{TM2TapeCost}{TM2TapeCost.source_step}
The bound includes the entry tick and growth from earlier pushes in the same
statement.
Both branches and finite local loads are covered.
The actual framed
request and consuming-response programs have $a\leq3$, giving $6H+25$ TM1 ticks
per transition, with all caller/private columns retained.
\companchor{BitOracleTapeCost}{BitOracleTapeCost.request_accesses}
\companchor{BitOracleTapeCost}{BitOracleTapeCost.response_accesses}
\companchor{BitOracleTapeCost}{BitOracleTapeCost.request_step}
\companchor{BitOracleTapeCost}{BitOracleTapeCost.response_step}

Five Lean controls construct a complete literal tape presentation, check a
16-tick peek/push, refute constant access cost, cover branch maxima and check
empty peek/pop with another column intact.
Independent tape fixtures cover
6,000 cases, statement depth at most 6, stack lengths 0--8 and seeds 7/19/41.
Full stack/tape results and the cost bound agree; skipped-return and cross-column
erasure mutants are detected.
Failures and discarded cases are zero.
\companchor{TM2TapeCostControls}{TM2TapeCostControls.derived_step}
\companchor{TM2TapeCostControls}{TM2TapeCostControls.actual_peek_push}
\companchor{TM2TapeCostControls}{TM2TapeCostControls.constant_cost_not_enough}
\companchor{TM2TapeCostControls}{TM2TapeCostControls.branch_bound}
\companchor{TM2TapeCostControls}{TM2TapeCostControls.empty_peek_pop}

These are TM1 tick bounds.
The generic simulation allows arbitrary local-memory
types; finite domains and reachable syntax must still justify primitive costs.
The next subsection derives packing and accumulated deterministic bounds.

\subsection{Historical component: Finite tape runs and canonical presentation}\label{sec:tm2-tape-runs}
Canonical packing reverses each source stack and aligns the columns at their
bottoms.
With $H=\max_k|S_k|$, column $k$ at position $i<H$ is
$\mathsf{reverse}(S_k)[i]?$; all later cells are blank.
This constructs
Mathlib's full \code{TrCfg} relation.
Column projections also show that two
related tape configurations for the same source configuration are equal.
\companchor{TM2TapeRuns}{TM2TapeRuns.length_le_height}
\companchor{TM2TapeRuns}{TM2TapeRuns.columns_nth}
\companchor{TM2TapeRuns}{TM2TapeRuns.columns_presentation}
\companchor{TM2TapeRuns}{TM2TapeRuns.pack_related}
\companchor{TM2TapeRuns}{TM2TapeRuns.related_unique}

\begin{theorem}[Finite execution from canonical tape data]
Let the stack and source-label types be finite, with decidable stack equality.
For source code $M$ and configuration $s$, put
$A=\max_\ell\mathsf{accesses}(M(\ell))$ and $H=\max_k|s.\mathsf{stk}(k)|$.
Take empty maxima as zero. For every $n$, there is $t$ such that
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 t&\leq n\bigl(1+A(2(H+nA)+2)\bigr),\\
 (\mathsf{tapeTick}_M)^t(\mathsf{pack}(s))
   &=\mathsf{pack}((\mathsf{sourceTick}_M)^n(s)).
 \end{aligned}
\]
Every final source stack has length at most $H+nA$.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{TM2TapeCost}{TM2TapeCost.statement_height}
\companchor{TM2TapeRuns}{TM2TapeRuns.run}
\companchor{TM2TapeRuns}{TM2TapeRuns.run_packed}
The proof derives intermediate growth, includes halt padding, and retains one
persistent tape across all transitions.
It requires no caller-supplied initial
presentation, height bound or code-bound certificate.

For the actual port programs, $A\leq3$.
A complete transfer taking at most $B$
source ticks therefore takes at most $B(6H+18B+7)$ tape ticks.
The outgoing
bound uses $B=|\mathit{oldPort}|+2|\mathit{request}|+4$; the response uses
$B=|\mathit{oldDestination}|+3|\mathit{answer}|+4$.
Here $H$ is the maximum
height of the actual starting transfer configuration.
The final request tape
contains the exact copied request and retained caller columns; the response
returns the actual resumed caller with both private columns empty.
\companchor{BitOracleTapeCost}{BitOracleTapeCost.request_run}
\companchor{BitOracleTapeCost}{BitOracleTapeCost.response_run}

Five Lean controls check derived and literal growing runs, early halt and
nonpalindromic packing.
Four pushes take $4+6+8+10=28$ ticks; the frozen-height
estimate of 20 stops inside the fourth push.
Unreversed packing returns the
wrong source top.
Independent persistent-tape fixtures cover 4,500 two-label
programs, depth at most 5, stack lengths 0--6, source fuel 0--8 and seeds
7/19/41.
Both mutants are detected; failures and discarded cases are zero.
\companchor{TM2TapeRunsControls}{TM2TapeRunsControls.derived_growing_run}
\companchor{TM2TapeRunsControls}{TM2TapeRunsControls.actual_growing_run}
\companchor{TM2TapeRunsControls}{TM2TapeRunsControls.frozen_height_fails}
\companchor{TM2TapeRunsControls}{TM2TapeRunsControls.packing_order_matters}
\companchor{TM2TapeRunsControls}{TM2TapeRunsControls.early_halt}

Packing specifies data, not an executed input loader.
These are deterministic
TM1 regions: native oracle I/O, finite reachable control and primitive costs,
scalar-handler policy and whole-algorithm PPT adequacy are outside this component's guarantee.
Generic
local-memory and symbol types need not be finite in this simulation theorem.

\subsection{Historical component: Physical outgoing query-tape transfer}\label{sec:oracle-tape-output}
\code{OracleTapeOutput.step} reads the bottom-first work column while writing
leftward on a separate query tape, restoring the original word order.
Four
finite phases clear the old query, copy, return the work head and finish.
Each
transition reads current cells, writes at most one query cell and moves each
head by at most one.
Work cells are never written.

Write $Q(w)$ for a tape with $w$ in ordinary list order, its head at the first
bit and all other cells blank; $Q([])$ is entirely blank.
Let $P(s)$ be the
work tape of the existing canonical packing of source configuration $s$.
\begin{theorem}[Outgoing export with physical query cleanup]
For a finite stack type with decidable equality, source configuration $s$,
selected stack $k$, and any previous native word $u$, put $w=s.\mathsf{stk}(k)$.
Then the actual head-local transition function satisfies
\[
 \mathsf{step}_k^{\,|u|+2|w|+3}
   (\mathsf{clear},P(s),Q(u))
   =(\mathsf{done},P(s),Q(w)).
\]
\end{theorem}
\companchor{OracleTapeOutput}{OracleTapeOutput.clear_run}
\companchor{OracleTapeOutput}{OracleTapeOutput.run}
\companchor{OracleTapeOutput}{OracleTapeOutput.run_packed}
\companchor{OracleTapeOutput}{OracleTapeOutput.run_replacing}
Clearing takes $|u|+1$ transitions and derives the blank query tape.
Export from that blank tape takes $2|w|+2$.
The equality restores the complete
work tape, including the bottom marker, head and every other column.
The
column and marker laws are derived from actual source data.

\code{BitOracleTapeOutput.request_run} connects this to the actual request
preparation.
With $B=|\mathit{oldPort}|+2|\mathit{request}|+4$ and initial
maximum height $H$, preparation uses at most $B(6H+18B+7)$ TM1 ticks.
The
subsequent head-local transfer uses exactly $|u|+2|\mathit{request}|+3$
transitions and produces $Q(\mathit{request})$, preserving the complete prepared
work tape.
These are two derived region executions, not yet one compiled
oracle-dispatch loop or a combined standard-machine cost theorem.
\companchor{BitOracleTapeOutput}{BitOracleTapeOutput.request_run}

Seven Lean controls cover exact and literal export, empty words, a longer
caller column, and wrong-direction, skipped-return and stale-suffix failures.
Clearing repairs the stale suffix.
Independent two-head fixtures cover 5,400
cases, each with fresh and reused query tapes, 1--5 work columns, word lengths
0--20 and seeds 7/19/41.
All three mutants are detected; failures and discarded
cases are zero.
\companchor{OracleTapeOutputControls}{OracleTapeOutputControls.derived_export}
\companchor{OracleTapeOutputControls}{OracleTapeOutputControls.actual_export}
\companchor{OracleTapeOutputControls}{OracleTapeOutputControls.rightward_write_fails}
\companchor{OracleTapeOutputControls}{OracleTapeOutputControls.return_is_required}
\companchor{OracleTapeOutputControls}{OracleTapeOutputControls.empty_export}
\companchor{OracleTapeOutputControls}{OracleTapeOutputControls.stale_query_fails}
\companchor{OracleTapeOutputControls}{OracleTapeOutputControls.stale_query_cleaned}

The previous native tape must be $Q(u)$, including its head position; cleanup
is now executed for every such prior output.
Response-tape loading, native
query dispatch, physical input loading, finite-control/primitive-cost adequacy
for the full compiled loop and the actual scalar-handler contract are outside this component's guarantee.
No protocol observation or cryptographic assumption changed.

\subsection{Historical component: Physical answer loading and response execution}\label{sec:oracle-tape-input}
\code{OracleTapeInput.step} replaces the private work column with a native
answer.
Eight finite phases scan and erase the old column, scan the answer,
read its bits backward while pushing them at the column top, and return both
heads.
Each transition reads current cells, writes at most one work cell and
moves each head by at most one.
The proofs reuse Mathlib's existing push/pop
tape-update theorem.
Other columns and the bottom marker are retained.

Let $P(s)$ be the work tape of the existing canonical source packing and
$Q(a)$ the ordinary native answer tape, at its first bit with all other cells
blank.
Write $s[k:=a]$ for replacement of only source stack $k$.
\begin{theorem}[Physical answer replacement]
For a finite stack type with decidable equality, source configuration $s$,
stack $k$ and native answer $a$, the actual transition function satisfies
\[
 \mathsf{step}_k^{\,3|s.\mathsf{stk}(k)|+4|a|+6}
   (\mathsf{seekWork},P(s),Q(a))
   =(\mathsf{done},P(s[k:=a]),Q(a)).
\]
\end{theorem}
\companchor{OracleTapeInput}{OracleTapeInput.run_packed}
The initial private word need not be empty.
The conclusion identifies both
complete tapes and both restored head positions.
Erasure removes old suffixes
when the answer is shorter or empty; backward native reading preserves source
stack order.
No blank-port or structural-presentation certificate is assumed.

\code{BitOracleTapeInput.load_request} applies the loader to the actual
completed request configuration, deriving the exact work tape expected by
\code{BitOraclePortTransfer.start}.
It uses the existing delivery/layout
identity to preserve every caller column and scratch port.
\companchor{BitOracleTapeInput}{BitOracleTapeInput.load_request}
\code{BitOracleTapeInput.response_run} follows this with the actual consuming
response program.
Loading takes $3|\mathit{oldPort}|+4|a|+6$ head-local
transitions; response execution takes at most $B(6H+18B+7)$ TM1 ticks, where
$B=|\mathit{oldDestination}|+3|a|+4$ and $H$ is the response-start maximum
stack height.
The final source configuration is halted, projects to the actual
resumed caller, and has both private ports empty.
These remain separate region
executions and cost units until the compiled native-dispatch loop is assembled.
\companchor{BitOracleTapeInput}{BitOracleTapeInput.response_run}

Six Lean controls check exact and literal shorter replacement, an empty answer,
nonpalindromic order and a longer caller column.
Mutants omit erasure, scan away
from the answer or write to a caller column.
Independent two-head fixtures cover
5,400 cases, 2--5 work columns, old/answer lengths 0--16 and seeds 7/19/41.
They check full column replacement, retained answer, both heads and the exact
count; all three mutants are detected, with zero failures or discarded cases.
\companchor{OracleTapeInputControls}{OracleTapeInputControls.derived_load}
\companchor{OracleTapeInputControls}{OracleTapeInputControls.actual_load}

\companchor{OracleTapeInputControls}{OracleTapeInputControls.other_column_fails}
\companchor{OracleTapeInputControls}{OracleTapeInputControls.empty_answer}

The native answer must have the stated canonical word/head layout.
The next
section supplies native dispatch and per-call composition.
Whole-source-loop
composition, physical initial-input loading, finite-control and primitive-cost
adequacy, and the scalar-handler contract are outside this component's guarantee.
The protocol, attacker observations and cryptographic assumptions are unchanged.

\subsection{Historical component: Native oracle dispatch and complete call execution}\label{sec:oracle-tape-call}
Native decoding stops at the first blank, is well-defined on the trailing-blank
quotient, and satisfies $\mathsf{readWord}(Q(w))=w$.
Hash/coin dispatch invokes
the original query, retains work/query tapes and supplies the canonical answer.
Whole-word interpretation belongs to the native event; local transfers execute
the physical steps above.
\companchor{OracleTapeDispatch}{OracleTapeDispatch.read_wordTape}
\companchor{OracleTapeDispatch}{OracleTapeDispatch.hash_word}
\companchor{OracleTapeDispatch}{OracleTapeDispatch.coin_word}

The loop combines outgoing steps, one native event, incoming steps and actual
TM1 response ticks.
Handoffs retain tapes and finite metadata.
First-completion
proofs stop pure phases before another query; the completed state is absorbing.

Fix a raw caller $c$ with finite port/label/memory indices, request and destination
ports $r,d$ (possibly equal), next label $\ell$, $w=c.\mathsf{stk}(r)$, previous
canonical query $Q(v)$ and arbitrary old answer tape.
Let $\mathsf{begin}$ be
export entry on the actual prepared work tape; $\mathsf{incoming}(a)$ starts
loading the native reply while retaining $Q(w)$.
\begin{theorem}[Native call boundaries]
There is $b\le |v|+2|w|+5$, chosen before the answer, such that
\[
 \mathsf{run}_{\mathsf{hash}}(b,\mathsf{begin})
 =\bigl(a\leftarrow\mathsf{hash}(w);\ \mathsf{return}\ \mathsf{incoming}(a)\bigr).
\]
For every raw answer $a$, let $B=|c.\mathsf{stk}(d)|+3|a|+4$ and let $H$ be
the maximum stack height of the actual response-start configuration. There
are $t\le 3|w|+4|a|+8+B(6H+18B+7)$ and a halted transfer configuration $o$
with both private stacks empty and projection
$\mathsf{resume}(c,d,\ell,a)$, such that
\[
 \mathsf{run}_{\mathsf{hash}}(t,\mathsf{incoming}(a))
 =\mathsf{return}\ \mathsf{done}(\mathsf{pack}(o),Q(w),Q(a)).
\]
\end{theorem}
\companchor{BitOracleTapeCall}{BitOracleTapeCall.hash_issue}
\companchor{BitOracleTapeCall}{BitOracleTapeCall.answer_run}
These query-tree equalities retain every answer and complete final tapes.
\code{received_run} allows any old private word/query tape, replacing $|w|$
by that old word's length in the bound; \code{coin_issue} supplies its bit reply.
\companchor{BitOracleTapeCall}{BitOracleTapeCall.received_run}
\companchor{BitOracleTapeCall}{BitOracleTapeCall.coin_issue}
\code{hash_prepared_issue} derives the export entry from the actual request
program's TM1 run, with its existing bound $B_0(6H_0+18B_0+7)$, where
$B_0=|\mathit{oldPort}|+2|w|+4$ and $H_0$ is its initial maximum stack height.
No caller supplies a presentation or execution certificate.
\companchor{BitOracleTapeCall}{BitOracleTapeCall.hash_prepared_issue}
\companchor{BitOracleTapeCall}{BitOracleTapeCall.run_add}

Seven Lean controls check preparation, silent/issue boundaries, actual
hash/cache-hit/coin runs and first-blank termination.
Independent fixtures cover
2,880 three-call cache interactions (seeds 7/19/41, lengths 0--8) and 2,000
prefix cases.
They check physical transfers and source cache/log results,
not full TM1 timing.
The blank-skipping mutant is detected; failures/gaveUp=0.
\companchor{OracleTapeCallControls}{OracleTapeCallControls.hash_call}
\companchor{OracleTapeCallControls}{OracleTapeCallControls.occupied_reply}
\companchor{OracleTapeCallControls}{OracleTapeCallControls.coin_call}
\companchor{OracleTapeCallControls}{OracleTapeCallControls.blank_terminates}

\subsection{Historical component: Finite source execution in the tape loop}\label{sec:oracle-tape-loop}
\code{BitOracleTapeLoop} retains explicit work/query/answer tapes through
ordinary computation, request preparation and the existing call transitions.
Returns change finite caller label/memory only.
To regain control after one
ordinary statement, the adapter saves its resulting label and halts; goto
stores \code{some label}, source halt stores \code{none}.
Existing relocation
and TM1 execution proofs derive the source result and private frame.
\companchor{BitOracleTapeCompute}{BitOracleTapeCompute.run}

Let $F_n(c)$ be the original source run for $n$ instructions, projected to its
caller result (discarding ghost charge).
Write $\mathsf{ready}(c,Q(v),A)$ for
its canonical work tape, including both empty private columns, and the stated
native tapes.
The query-tree relation $R$ means:
\[
\begin{aligned}
 R(\mathsf{return}\ c,s)&\iff
   \exists t,v,A.\ \mathsf{run}(t,s)=\mathsf{return}\ \mathsf{ready}(c,Q(v),A),\\
 R(\mathsf{query}(q,k),s)&\iff
   \exists t,\kappa.\ \mathsf{run}(t,s)=\mathsf{query}(q,\mathsf{return}\circ\kappa)
   \ \land\ \forall a.\ R(k(a),\kappa(a)).
\end{aligned}
\]
\begin{theorem}[Finite source correspondence]
For every fixed raw source code with finite port, label and memory types,
source caller $c$, finite fuel $n$, previous query word $v$ and arbitrary old
answer tape $A$,
\[
 R(F_n(c),\mathsf{ready}(c,Q(v),A)).
\]
Every path of $F_n(c)$ occurs in an actual finite tape run with the same complete
query/answer log and final caller, including its canonical work presentation
and empty private columns.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{BitOracleTapeCorrespondence}{BitOracleTapeLoop.run_source}
\companchor{BitOracleTapeCorrespondence}{BitOracleTapeLoop.realizes_path}
The proof derives all successor presentation and execution witnesses.
Source
fuel may stop at a live caller; the stopping times may depend on answers.
This statement establishes neither a common clock nor polynomial runtime.

Each ordinary statement has derived loop bound $3+a(2(H+a)+2)$, with $a$ its
stack-access count and $H$ its framed initial height.
Hash dispatch, two-step
coin dispatch and complete reply/return retain their actual native events and
region bounds.
Their initial states follow from source dispatch; pure-region
lifting reuses the call proofs before returning to the outer loop.
\companchor{BitOracleTapeLoop}{BitOracleTapeLoop.compute_run}
\companchor{BitOracleTapeLoop}{BitOracleTapeLoop.hash_issue}
\companchor{BitOracleTapeLoop}{BitOracleTapeLoop.coin_issue}
\companchor{BitOracleTapeLoop}{BitOracleTapeLoop.reply_run}

Eight Lean controls check original source expectations, actual mixed local/
three-call execution, occupied caches, aliased ports, coins and goto/halt
failures.
They inspect complete caller/private words, native words and traces.
An independent source/finite-return interpreter campaign covers 3,600 cases,
seeds 7/19/41, depths 0--6, 1--3 ports and word lengths 0--8; both return mutants
are detected, with zero failures/gaveUp.
This campaign does not measure TM1 timing.
\companchor{OracleTapeLoopControls}{OracleTapeLoopControls.mixed_run}
\companchor{OracleTapeLoopControls}{OracleTapeLoopControls.occupied_run}
\companchor{OracleTapeLoopControls}{OracleTapeLoopControls.goto_not_halt}
\companchor{OracleTapeLoopControls}{OracleTapeLoopControls.halt_clears_label}

\subsection{Historical component: Source-derived polynomial tape clocks}\label{sec:oracle-tape-bounded}
The finite path result is strengthened to one clock for every permitted answer
branch.
Let $P$ be fixed source code, $c$ its initial caller, $f$ source fuel,
$L$ a hash-answer length bound and $B$ a source charge bound.
Write $H$ for
$c$'s maximum word length, $V$ for the previous native query's length, and
$Q(w)$ for the canonical native word tape.
The old answer tape $A$ is arbitrary.
The existing adapter $\alpha_L$ admits every raw request, both coin values and
exactly hash answers of length at most $L$.
The premise
$\mathsf{Within}_L(B,\mathsf{Source.run}(P,f,c))$ requires every allowed branch
to halt with total source charge at most $B$.

\begin{theorem}[Common-clock source correspondence; machine-checked]
Under that source premise, put $T=650B(H+B+V+1)^2$. In compressed notation,
\[
\begin{aligned}
 &\mathsf{Obs}\mathbin{<\$>}
   \mathsf{simulate}(\alpha_L,
     \mathsf{Tape.run}(P,T,\mathsf{ready}(c,Q(w),A)))\\
 &\qquad=(\mathsf{some}\circ\mathsf{fst})\mathbin{<\$>}
   \mathsf{simulate}(\alpha_L,\mathsf{Source.run}(P,f,c)),
   \qquad |w|=V.
\end{aligned}
\]
Here $\mathsf{Obs}$ interprets every original caller word at a ready boundary
and returns $\mathsf{none}$ in unfinished phases. This equality of oracle
computations also preserves all effects of every lawful bounded-oracle handler.
\end{theorem}

\companchor{BitOracleTapeBounded}{BitOracleTapeLoop.run_source_handler}

\subsection{Historical component: Primitive costs through the existing compiler}\label{sec:tm1-primitive}
Mathlib's TM1-to-TM0 compiler translates each statement into physical single
moves/writes.
It folds loads and branches on the finite head symbol and local
memory into its transition table.
Goto and halt emit a final identity write.
For statement $q$, let $a(q)$ count the maximum moves/writes plus that final
write along a syntactic branch.
Let $w(q,v,T)$ count the actual branch on
memory $v$ and tape $T$.

\begin{theorem}[Primitive statement and supported-run bounds; machine-checked]
For every TM1 program $M$, statement $q$, memory $v$ and tape $T$,
\[
\begin{gathered}
 w(q,v,T)\le a(q),\\
 \mathsf{tick}_0(\mathsf{tr}(M))^{w(q,v,T)}
     ((\mathsf{some}\ q,v),T)
   =\mathsf{trCfg}_M(\mathsf{stepAux}_1(q,v,T)).
\end{gathered}
\]
If finite $S$ supports $M$ and the initial caller label belongs to
$\mathsf{insertNone}(S)$, put $K=\max_{\ell\in S}a(M(\ell))$. Then every
$n$-tick TM1 run has some $t\le nK$ with exact compiled final state:
\[
 \mathsf{tick}_0(\mathsf{tr}(M))^t(\mathsf{trCfg}_M(c))
   =\mathsf{trCfg}_M(\mathsf{tick}_1(M)^n(c)).
\]
Ticks stutter after halt. Support includes the entry label and is closed under
all statement jumps. The displayed notation compresses the pinned translations.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{TM1PrimitiveCost}{TM1PrimitiveCost.statement}
\companchor{TM1PrimitiveCost}{TM1PrimitiveCost.work_le}
\companchor{TM1PrimitiveCost}{TM1PrimitiveCost.run}

For the actual returning computation and request/response programs, both pinned
compiler support proofs derive finite successor-closed primitive control from
finite source labels and local memory.
Canonical source entries derive support
membership.
Thus each prior complete region bound is multiplied by a constant
$K$ determined solely by its fixed code, with no caller support/cost certificate.
All work-tape columns and saved memory agree; response execution still yields
the resumed caller and both empty private columns.
\companchor{BitOraclePrimitiveCost}{BitOraclePrimitiveCost.finite_support}
\companchor{BitOraclePrimitiveCost}{BitOraclePrimitiveCost.compute_run}
\companchor{BitOraclePrimitiveCost}{BitOraclePrimitiveCost.request_run}
\companchor{BitOraclePrimitiveCost}{BitOraclePrimitiveCost.response_run}

\subsection{Historical component: Finite execution in the primitive oracle loop}\label{sec:primitive-loop}
The primitive target retains the existing physical output/input operations and
native oracle dispatch.
Computation, preparation and response use the pinned
primitive compiler; phase returns inspect its halt control and saved caller
memory.
Lowering $\ell_P$ preserves every tape and flattens the old terminal
pure call dispatch into its ready state, allowing a zero-step match there.
The primitive wrapper is proved equal to the pinned compiler tick.
\companchor{BitOraclePrimitiveLoop}{BitOraclePrimitiveLoop.primitiveTick_eq}

Write $S_P(c)$ for membership of each old deterministic residual label in its
compiler-derived finite support.
Other phases already have finite tags and
memory.
The fixed factor $G_P$ is one plus the sum of code-factor maxima over
source labels, request ports and response ports.
Ready states derive $S_P$;
every old-loop successor preserves it for every raw native answer, and each
supported action matches within $G_P$ primitive-loop transitions.
\companchor{BitOraclePrimitiveSupport}{BitOraclePrimitiveLoop.step_supported}
\companchor{BitOraclePrimitiveSupport}{BitOraclePrimitiveLoop.run_supported}
\companchor{BitOraclePrimitiveSupport}{BitOraclePrimitiveLoop.supported_step_match}

The relation $C_P(o,B,s)$ exposes complete query-tree refinement:
\[
\begin{aligned}
 C_P(\mathsf{return}\ c,B,s)&\iff
   \exists t\le B.\ \mathsf{run}_P(t,s)=\mathsf{return}\ \ell_P(c),\\
 C_P(\mathsf{query}(q,k),B,s)&\iff
   \exists t\le B,\kappa.\ \mathsf{run}_P(t,s)
       =\mathsf{query}(q,\mathsf{return}\circ\kappa)\\
 &\hspace{20mm}{}\land\ \forall a.\ C_P(k(a),B-t,\kappa(a)).
\end{aligned}
\]
\begin{theorem}[Bounded finite primitive refinement; machine-checked]
For every fixed source program $P$, old tape-loop state $c$ and finite fuel $n$,
\[
 S_P(c)\Longrightarrow
 C_P(\mathsf{Tape.run}(P,n,c),nG_P,\ell_P(c)).
\]
For a canonical source ready entry, support is derived automatically. The
conclusion retains all native query/answer branches and complete lowered leaf
states, with the accumulated bound $nG_P$ on each branch.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{BitOraclePrimitiveCorrespondence}{BitOraclePrimitiveLoop.run_tape}
\companchor{BitOraclePrimitiveCorrespondence}{BitOraclePrimitiveLoop.run_ready}

\subsection{Historical component: Finite target control and primitive source clocks}\label{sec:primitive-contract}
The target invariant now covers intermediate residual instructions in the
pinned compiler's finite supports.
Every primitive/native successor preserves
it for arbitrary raw answers.
Erasing only the three tapes defines a control
projection $\pi$; a code-derived finite set $F_P$ contains $\pi(c)$ for every
reachable target state $c$, independently of input size, answer size and fuel.
Reconstruction proves that no other state was dropped:
\[
 \mathsf{withTapes}(\mathsf{tapes}(c),\pi(c))=c.
\]
Both work and native alphabets are finite.
These projections are proof-side
representation facts, not new runtime operations.
\companchor{BitOraclePrimitiveFinite}{BitOraclePrimitiveFinite.reachable_control}
\companchor{BitOraclePrimitiveFinite}{BitOraclePrimitiveFinite.reconstruct}
\companchor{BitOraclePrimitiveFinite}{BitOraclePrimitiveFinite.alphabets_finite}

\begin{theorem}[Derived primitive source clock; machine-checked]
Fix source code $P$, initial caller $c$, source fuel $f$, answer bound $L$ and
source charge bound $B$. Assume the original all-allowed-branch contract
$\mathsf{Within}_L(B,\mathsf{Source.run}(P,f,c))$: every permitted source leaf
halts with charge at most $B$. With $H$ the initial maximum word length,
previous native query $w$ of length $V$, arbitrary old answer tape $A$, and
fixed code factor $G_P$, put $T=650G_PB(H+B+V+1)^2$. In compressed notation,
\[
\begin{aligned}
 &\mathsf{Obs}\mathbin{<\$>}
   \mathsf{simulate}(\alpha_L,
     \mathsf{Primitive.run}(P,T,\ell_P(\mathsf{ready}(c,Q(w),A))))\\
 &\qquad=(\mathsf{some}\circ\mathsf{fst})\mathbin{<\$>}
   \mathsf{simulate}(\alpha_L,\mathsf{Source.run}(P,f,c)).
\end{aligned}
\]
The unchanged adapter $\alpha_L$ allows every raw request, both coin values
and hash answers of length at most $L$. The equality preserves every lawful
bounded-oracle handler's complete effects.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{BitOraclePrimitiveBounded}{BitOraclePrimitiveBounded.run_source_bounded}
\companchor{BitOraclePrimitiveBounded}{BitOraclePrimitiveBounded.run_source_handler}

The proof derives both primitive correspondence and halt of the intermediate

Padding is justified only after
halt.
$\mathsf{Obs}$ interprets all original caller words at ready boundaries;

The adaptive
$H(x),H(H(x)),H(x)$ canary derives halt and $B=4+2|x|+4L$ itself.

\subsection{Historical component: Physical initial raw-input loading}\label{sec:initial-input}
Fix code $P$, an original input port $i$, initial label and finite memory.
The
initial caller $c_x$ has the raw word $x$ on port $i$ and every other port empty.
The executable prelude starts with blank working storage and a native input
tape $Q(x)$.
It writes the bottom-marker cell, reuses the physical input loader
on $i$, and hands its actual tapes to the primitive loop using finite control.
Canonical packing occurs only in the proof statement.

\begin{theorem}[Physical startup and source transport; machine-checked]
For every raw bitstring $x$, there exists $u\leq4|x|+8$ such that
\[
 \mathsf{Startup.run}(P,u,\mathsf{blank},Q(x))
 =\mathsf{pure}(\mathsf{running}(\mathsf{ready}(c_x,Q([]),Q(x)))).
\]
Here ready denotes the canonical full work tape with both private columns
empty; the input tape and head are restored. Assume additionally
$\mathsf{Within}_L(B,\mathsf{Source.run}(P,f,c_x))$, the original source
halt/charge contract for all allowed answers. Put
$T=650G_PB(H+B+1)^2$, where $H$ is the maximum initial source word length.
For a derived $u\leq4|x|+8$, in compressed notation,
\[
\begin{aligned}
 &\mathsf{Obs}\mathbin{<\$>}
   \mathsf{simulate}(\alpha_L,
     \mathsf{Startup.run}(P,u+T,\mathsf{blank},Q(x)))\\
 &\qquad=(\mathsf{some}\circ\mathsf{fst})\mathbin{<\$>}
   \mathsf{simulate}(\alpha_L,\mathsf{Source.run}(P,f,c_x)).
\end{aligned}
\]
This equality transports every lawful bounded-oracle handler's complete effects.
The derived startup time is shared by the entire query tree, independently of
oracle answers. No presentation or intermediate correspondence premise is added.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{BitOracleInitialInput}{BitOracleInitialInput.load}
\companchor{BitOracleInitialInput}{BitOracleInitialInput.run_running}
\companchor{BitOracleInitialInput}{BitOracleInitialInput.run_source_bounded}
\companchor{BitOracleInitialInput}{BitOracleInitialInput.run_source_handler}

\subsection{Historical component: Local standard-tape computation}\label{sec:bit-tape-coverage}
The converse local-computation obligation uses the pinned TM0 model with
alphabet $\{\bot,0,1\}$ and a fixed nonempty finite set of labels.
Two existing
bit stacks store the cells on either side, nearest the current head first.
Each stored cell has two bits: $\bot\mapsto00$, $0\mapsto10$, $1\mapsto11$.
A three-valued finite register stores the current cell.
Missing cells mean
blank, not the data bit zero.
Every native tape has such finite representatives;
choosing them is proof-side existence, not executed input conversion.
\companchor{BitTapeCoverage}{BitTapeCoverage.represents_every_tape}
\companchor{BitTapeCoverage}{BitTapeCoverage.cells_length}

\begin{theorem}[Native local computation in bit source; machine-checked]
Fix a native program $M$ and a represented configuration $c$. Let $E(c)$ be
its bit-source representation, $P_M$ its compiled source program, and $N_M$
the native transition with an explicit terminal label. At a live label,
$N_M$ agrees with Mathlib's $\mathsf{TM0.step}$; when it returns no successor,
$N_M$ records halt and retains the final tape. For every $f$,
\[
 \exists a\leq7f,\qquad
 \mathsf{Source.run}(P_M,f,E(c))
   =\mathsf{pure}(E(N_M^{[f]}(c)),a).
\]
Every finite run preserves both full tape sides and the current cell.
Each compiled statement has source charge at most seven. If $N_M^{[f]}(c)$
halts, this derives the original source contract with $B=7f$. The existing
primitive loop then returns $E(N_M^{[f]}(c))$ at clock
$650G_{P_M}B(H+B+1)^2$, with $H$ the initial source height and empty initial
native query/answer tapes. The primitive result is observed at the ready
boundary using the same proof-side full-caller observation as before.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{BitTapeCoverage}{BitTapeCoverage.native_step}
\companchor{BitTapeCoverage}{BitTapeCoverage.source_run}
\companchor{BitTapeCoverage}{BitTapeCoverage.charged_run}
\companchor{BitTapeCoverage}{BitTapeCoverage.local_cost}
\companchor{BitTapeCoverage}{BitTapeCoverage.within}
\companchor{BitTapeCoverage}{BitTapeCoverage.primitive_run}

Six controls check a complete write/move run, its live prefix, independent
pinned TM0 execution, motion into blank storage, blank-versus-zero branching
and the derived cost.
The complete fixture has blank at the head, zero one
cell left and one two cells left.
Independent sparse-tape and encoded-stack

\subsection{Historical component: Physical input through native local execution}\label{sec:bit-tape-input}
The input prelude uses the same two bit stacks and three-valued cell register.
It reverses raw bits onto the left workspace, inserts present-cell tags while
moving them back, then extracts the current head.
This takes exactly
$2|x|+3$ source transitions for every raw word $x$.
The left stack ends empty;
the head is the first bit (blank for empty input), and the right stack contains
the remaining cells in their original order.
No encoded cells are supplied.
\companchor{BitTapeInput}{BitTapeInput.run}

The existing return linker connects the three conversion phases to arbitrary
fixed native code.
A separate halt label precedes the relocated native labels,
so a native return cannot restart conversion.
Conversion statements have
source charge at most five; the linked program's bound is seven.
The generic
deterministic charge induction also supplies the preceding native coverage
result, replacing its duplicate specialized induction.
\companchor{BitTapeStart}{BitTapeStart.convert_run}
\companchor{BitTapeStart}{BitTapeStart.charged_run}
\companchor{BitOracleMachine}{BitOracleMachine.compute_run_cost}

\begin{theorem}[Physical raw input to native result; machine-checked]
Fix a TM0 program $M$ over blank/false/true cells with a nonempty finite label
set, entry label $q$, and raw input $x$. Let $c_x$ be the ordinary native input
configuration with head on the first bit. Assume $N_M^{[f]}(c_x)$ halts.
For the linked source code $P$, put
\[
 B=7(2|x|+f+4),\qquad T=650G_PB(|x|+B+1)^2.
\]
There exists $u\leq4|x|+8$ such that, in compressed notation,
\[
\begin{aligned}
 &\mathsf{Obs}\mathbin{<\$>}
  \mathsf{simulate}(\alpha_L,
    \mathsf{Startup.run}(P,u+T,\mathsf{blank},Q(x)))\\
 &\qquad=\mathsf{pure}(\mathsf{some}(E_P(N_M^{[f]}(c_x)))).
\end{aligned}
\]
Here $E_P$ retains the complete represented native tape and relocates its
labels into $P$. This holds for every bounded-answer adapter $\alpha_L$.
The linked source is deterministic and its complete query tree is pure.
The original native halt bound derives source termination, charge, initial
presentation and conversion correctness; none is a separate caller certificate.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{BitTapeStart}{BitTapeStart.physical_run}

Seven controls check conversion and its prefix, linked source execution,

head becomes true under native execution; its tail remains unchanged.
