The combined controller consists of the original raw first-proof prefix, reentry,
a framed copy of the complete executor, reentry, and a second framed copy of the
same executor definition.
This shares code and proof definitions; it is not a
general call stack or one shared physical instruction block.
Its independent
native fixture uses $p=3,q=2,g=2,pk=1$, true vote, $r_1=r_2=1$ and total nonce
zero.
All resident words, state/history, 26 consumed coins and residual tape are
checked.
Mutations break the total return or omit the second component's state
update.
The identity public key is a valid typed constructor input, not an
honest nonzero-secret key-generation trace.
This bounded validation is independent of the general run theorem below.

\begin{theorem}[Two distinct requests from original raw input]
Let $p,q$ be prime, $g,pk$ typed group inputs, $v$ Boolean, $s,L$ arbitrary finite
initial programmed/live states and $\sigma\in\mathbb N$. Let
$I=\mathsf{input}(g,pk,\sigma,v,\mathsf{saved}(s,L))$. Write $T_0,B_0$ for the
existing first-proof raw caller's clock and charge bound on $I$, and $T_Q(N),B_Q(N)$
for the complete resident request bounds with both state-size parameters set to
$N$. Define
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 H_0&=|I|+B_0,\\
 H_1&=H_0+B_R(H_0)+B_Q(H_0),\\
 T&=T_0+T_R(H_0)+T_Q(H_0)+T_R(H_1)+T_Q(H_1),\\
 B&=B_0+B_R(H_0)+B_Q(H_0)+B_R(H_1)+B_Q(H_1),
 \end{aligned}
\]
where $T_R(N)=T_R(q,N)$ and $B_R(N)=32T_R(q,N)$. The fixed combined controller
$M$ satisfies
\[
 \pi_{\mathrm{cfg}}\bigl(\mathsf{run}(M,T,\mathsf{start}(I))\bigr)
 =\mathsf{simulateQ}(\mathsf{liftCoins})
   \bigl(R\mathbin{<\$>}\mathsf{runFairBitUniform}_{\sigma}(D)\bigr).
\]
Here $D$ draws the original nonce-pair/first-proof tuple, then fresh
$(c,e,z_0,z_1)$ for p1, then a fresh such tuple for the total proof, in that order.
$R$ is the full configuration defined by \code{PrimeRemainingProofCaller.sourceResult}.
Every supported result halts and has actual charge at most $B$.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{PrimeRemainingProofCallerRun}{PrimeRemainingProofCaller.execution_source}
\companchor{PrimeRemainingProofCallerRun}{PrimeRemainingProofCaller.run_support}
\companchor{PrimeRemainingProofCallerRun}{PrimeRemainingProofCaller.linked_run}
The display compresses the clock/cost definitions and the nested tuple in
\code{drawSource}.
Both resident bounds follow from actual supported executions;
no frame, source-correspondence or cost certificate is supplied as a premise.
The original raw-input start has blank work and memory 0.
Reentry executes both
nonce presentations, including a zero sum, and retains the current cache,
sticky flag, duplicate-preserving history and separate live state.

Specializing the original two \code{ballotProofQuery} calls to return their proofs
gives exactly the same two-transcript source body and successor state.
Final
ports 48, 52 and 53 encode the original p0, p1 and total proofs; port 49 encodes
that successor state paired with unchanged live cache.
Earlier ciphertexts and
nonces are retained.
These claims use the original proof and state codecs.
\companchor{PrimeRemainingProofSource}{PrimeRemainingProofSource.source_run}
\companchor{PrimeRemainingProofSource}{PrimeRemainingProofSource.endpoint_outputs}
For every bounded-adapter limit, the same controller has physical startup at
most $4|I|+8$ and remaining clock
$\mathsf{unitCost}(|I|+B)\,B\,\mathsf{globalFactor}(M)$.
The physical observation
matches the same full source under the same bounded adapter on both sides.
This is the existing physical correspondence interface, not standard-PPT
coverage of all election attackers.
\companchor{PrimeRemainingProofCallerRun}{PrimeRemainingProofCaller.physical_run}
This component leaves the original \code{afterAliceProofs} continuation explicit and unexecuted: it does not establish runtime bounds for Alice verification/submission, Bob construction, attacker execution or final submission. Its fair-bit sampling loss is unchanged. The formal reference states the complete reduction theorem under external efficiency assumptions.

\paragraph{Limits of the historical implementation strategy.}
The historical implementation used the finite-machine representation and resident executor.
The request's arithmetic, commitment, programming and
serialization proofs are reused.
These components do not establish a complete compiler or a polynomial-time implementation of the whole reduction.


For two fixed calls, bounding successor tape height by current height plus paid
execution cost is sufficient.
Iterating a polynomial-in-height bound polynomially
many times need not remain polynomial.
Full reduction efficiency requires
structural cache/history/replay-state bounds.
Query counts alone cannot bound
arbitrary host computation.
The next section records the historical 90-minute feasibility experiment on the native compilation boundary.


\subsection{Historical component: A bounded native adaptive compilation test}
\label{sec:native-adaptive-compilation}
The historical 90-minute experiment tested whether an actual finite native oracle program
can be translated mechanically while preserving adaptive queries, independent
private work and execution cost.
A falsifier is a changed second query, a lost
private cell, or a charge unsupported by the executed target.

The native program reads only finite labels and current tape cells.
It hashes
an input word $x$, branches on the leading bit of the reply $a$ (empty means
false), reads a coin $c$, and hashes
\[
 d(x,a,c)=[\mathsf{headD}(a,\mathsf{false}),c]\mathbin{+\!+}
             \mathsf{drop}_2(x).
\]
The branch bit is written to the query tape before the coin replaces the answer
tape.
The private work tape is untouched.
The source halts after eight steps;
the nine-step validation clock adds one absorbing halted step.
\companchor{NativeAdaptiveProbe}{NativeAdaptiveProbe.exact_run}
\companchor{NativeAdaptiveProbe}{NativeAdaptiveProbe.padded_run}

Write $Z(b,w)$ for a tape whose finite left and inclusive right halves are
$b,w$, nearest cells first, with blanks beyond them.
Write $T(x)=Z([],x)$.
The mechanical translation $P=\mathsf{code}(\mathsf{NativeAdaptiveProbe.code})$
uses six stacks for the three pairs of halves.
It reads all three heads before
selecting the native command, then retains that finite selection while executing
its actions.
There is no separately supplied target implementation.
Its loaded start $I(b,w,x,o)$ has memory 0 and stacks
$[b,w,[],x,[],o]$.
Let $R(b,w,x,a,c,z)$ be its successful halted state, memory 0,
with stacks $[b,w,[],d(x,a,c),[],z]$.

\begin{theorem}[Fixed-program native correspondence and exact charge]
For arbitrary Boolean words $b,w,x,o$, the generated execution is exactly
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \mathsf{run}(P,19,I(b,w,x,o))={}&
  \mathsf{hash}(x)\mathbin{>\!\!>\!=}\bigl(a\mapsto
  \mathsf{coin}\mathbin{>\!\!>\!=}\bigl(c\mapsto\\[-2pt]
 &\mathsf{hash}(d(x,a,c))\mathbin{>\!\!>\!=}\bigl(z\mapsto
  \mathsf{pure}(R(b,w,x,a,c,z),\kappa)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr),\\
 \kappa={}&66+|x|+|o|+2|a|+|d(x,a,c)|+|z|.
\end{aligned}
\]
Observing only successful target halts gives exactly the native eight-step
query tree and its complete final tapes
$(Z(b,w),T(d(x,a,c)),T(z))$. Both hash replies are arbitrary words in this
equality, including empty replies and their entire tails.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{NativeAdaptiveRun}{NativeAdaptiveRun.exact_run}
\companchor{NativeAdaptiveRun}{NativeAdaptiveRun.native_correspondence}
The display abbreviates the constructors of the exact oracle query tree.
Observation returns none for a running state or rejection memory, so an
unsupported or partial run cannot satisfy the displayed successful endpoint.
The charge is the sum of actual generated steps, including replacement of the
old answer.
It is not a supplied certificate.

\begin{theorem}[Bounded replies and physical execution]
For every $L\in\mathbb N$, all branches with $|a|,|z|\le L$ halt and satisfy
\[
 \kappa\le B:=68+2|x|+|o|+3L.
\]
For any previous physical query word $v$ and any previous answer tape, set
\[
 H=\mathsf{height}(I(b,w,x,o))+B+|v|,\qquad
 F=\mathsf{unitCost}(H)\,B\,\mathsf{globalFactor}(P).
\]
Starting from the existing primitive lowering of the resident ready state,
physical execution at clock $F$ has exactly the same query tree and observed
target configurations under the same bounded-reply adapter on both sides.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{NativeAdaptiveCost}{NativeAdaptiveCost.within}
\companchor{NativeAdaptiveCost}{NativeAdaptiveCost.physical_run}
The adapter permits both coin values and every hash reply of length at most
$L$; it does not alter requests or accepted reply data.
The physical start
already contains the six resident words.
Loading arbitrary external tape
representations into that state is outside this theorem.

\paragraph{Validation and explicit boundary.}
Independent full-state/event/charge checks cover all input words of length at
most 3, both reply words of length at most 1, both coins and three private
frames: 810 cases, plus three directed cases and four compiled source-instruction
mutations.
The source
also has three full-tape positives and four mutation controls.
They distinguish
wrong answer/work-head selection, omitted branching, lost coin dependence and work
corruption.
These bounded controls validate the stated fixtures; the general
equality above is a separate proof.

The dense representation deliberately rejects blank writes and moves that would
store a blank cell.
Checked counterexamples execute native blank erasure or a
move from a blank head successfully, while the generated target halts with
rejection memory 26.
Thus the translator is executable on arbitrary finite
native code, but its correctness theorem here covers only the fixed adaptive
program and derived reachable states.
\companchor{NativeWordCompilerBoundary}{NativeWordCompilerBoundary.blank_write_native}
\companchor{NativeWordCompilerBoundary}{NativeWordCompilerBoundary.blank_write_target}
\companchor{NativeWordCompilerBoundary}{NativeWordCompilerBoundary.right_blank_native}
\companchor{NativeWordCompilerBoundary}{NativeWordCompilerBoundary.right_blank_target}
\companchor{NativeWordCompilerBoundary}{NativeWordCompilerBoundary.rejection_not_success}


\subsection{Historical component: General native query export}
\label{sec:native-query-export}
This historical experiment isolates the query-export operation needed beyond
the dense fixed-program test.
It uses the existing three-symbol tape
representation: a cell is $\mathsf{none}$, $\mathsf{some\ false}$ or
$\mathsf{some\ true}$, and its stored pair is
$[\mathsf{isSome},\mathsf{getD\ false}]$.
Thus blank cells have an explicit
payload bit and remain distinguishable from false cells.
The earlier compiler's
blank-operation counterexamples remain valid.

Let $E$ encode a finite cell list by concatenating these pairs.
For any head
cell $h$, before/after lists $b,a$, and $n$ arbitrary private Boolean words $P_i$,
the resident exporter starts with register $\mathsf{cellCode}(h)$ and words
\[
 W_0=E(a),\quad W_1=W_2=[],\quad W_3=E(b),\quad W_{4+i}=P_i.
\]
This is the existing head-register/cell-side representation with blank output
and scratch, framed with independent private storage.
Define
$Q=\mathsf{readWord}(\mathsf{tape}(h,b,a))$, the original native query decoder.

\begin{theorem}[General preserving native query export]
For every such $h,b,a,P$, the fixed exporter runs from this resident start at
clock $2|a|+6$ to a successful halt, with an actual charge
$\chi\le12|a|+36$. Its complete result has the original head register and
all the original words, except $W_1=Q$; scratch $W_2$ is empty. No restriction
excludes interior or trailing blanks, and every private word is unchanged.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{NativeQueryExportFrame}{NativeQueryExportFrame.charged}
\companchor{NativeQueryExportFrame}{NativeQueryExportFrame.native_result}
\companchor{NativeQueryExportFrame}{NativeQueryExportFrame.native_storage}
\companchor{NativeQueryExportFrame}{NativeQueryExportFrame.private_retained}

The head's two encoded bits are prepended by executed instructions, then removed
to restore the register.
The preserving core stops before the first blank,
leaves that cell and the entire later suffix untouched, and restores the scanned
prefix while producing the raw output.
The native-decoder equality is proved
for arbitrary finite cell representatives.
The clock and charge follow from
these actual instructions; neither correspondence nor cost is a premise.
\companchor{NativeQueryExportAdapter}{NativeQueryExportAdapter.run}
\companchor{NativeQueryReadWord}{NativeQueryReadWord.readWord}

Independent core and actual-head campaigns each cover 1,521 exhaustive cases
and one longer case, with five and seven instruction mutations respectively.
They check full storage, output, scratch, head restoration and charge.
Checked
literal controls distinguish false from blank and retain the postblank suffix.
This general operation does not yet compile
arbitrary adaptive native programs.
The following subsection supplies reply import
and the complete one-oracle-command composition.
The theorem starts with resident encoded cells; it does not
execute arbitrary external input packing.
\companchor{NativeQueryExportControls}{NativeQueryExportControls.ActualHead.false_head_is_bit}
\companchor{NativeQueryExportControls}{NativeQueryExportControls.ActualHead.interior_blank_retains_suffix}

\subsection{Historical component: One general native oracle round trip}
\label{sec:native-oracle-roundtrip}
The next interface executes one oracle command of an arbitrary finite native
program $p$.
Let $q$ be its current label and $h$ its three head cells, with
\(p(q,h)=\mathsf{oracle}(k,q')\), where $k$ selects hash or coin.
Each work, query and answer tape has arbitrary finite before/after cell lists
$b_i,a_i$, including internal blanks.
The resident start stores their lossless
encodings in ports $2i,2i+1$, all three heads in finite memory, empty scratch
ports $6,7$, and $n$ arbitrary private words.
Command selection uses the original
heads before the exporter or importer changes local memory.

Write $Q=\mathsf{readWord}(h_1,b_1,a_1)$ and let $L$ bound hash-reply length;
coins still admit both Boolean replies, including when $L=0$.
Define
\[
 E=2|a_1|+6,\qquad I=2|b_2|+|Q|+2(L+1)+5,
\]
\[
 F=E+I+4,\qquad
 C=8+6E+\bigl(2+|Q|+|\mathsf{cells}(a_2)|+L+1\bigr)+5I.
\]
The executor performs one actual entry step, then at most $F$ further steps,
stopping on the next native entry.
This is a live code label, not a halt or a
host callback; stopping there prevents padding from executing the continuation.

\begin{theorem}[Full native oracle return, compressed interface]
For every such $p,q,h,b,a,L$ and private family $P$, the projected full-state
query tree of the framed executor equals
\[
 (r\mapsto R(q',h,b,a,r,P))
 \mathbin{<\$>}\mathsf{boundedCall}_{L}(k,Q).
\]
Every supported execution has accumulated instruction charge at most $C$.
Here $R$ is live at $q'$, preserves the complete work/query tapes and every
private word, replaces the entire old answer by the native word tape of $r$,
and clears both scratch ports and the local register. Its saved answer head
is $r.\mathsf{head?}$; the work/query heads are unchanged.
\end{theorem}
\companchor{NativeRoundTripTheorem}{NativeRoundTrip.framed_correspondence}
\companchor{NativeRoundTripTheorem}{NativeRoundTrip.framed_cost}
\companchor{NativeRoundTripCorrectness}{NativeRoundTrip.native_correspondence}

The proof equates the exact oracle tree and establishes the native successor
representation at every leaf.
A support-only actual-prefix lemma would not
establish this event correspondence.
Independent campaigns check 6,481 whole
calls with six mutations, and 2,206 importer cases with three mutations;
13 kernel controls and observer counterexamples supplement them.
These bounded
checks do not replace the quantified theorem.
The interface can serve later
native oracle calls with independent private storage.
